Figure 3.
Translation of the NFB machine into HOcore

In Figure
3
, we present the translation of the NFB
machine into HOcore, where we consider flags as channel
names. Configurations now contain a counter
n
, which is represented
by a message on
k
containing the value of
n
encoded as a
process. We use
\left\ldbrack.\right\rdbrack_{\mathsf{Int}}
to translate a natural number
n
into a
process

\underbrace{suc(\_).\ldots suc(\_)}_{n\text{ times}}.z(\_).\overline{init}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}
; the role of the final output on
init
is
explained later. Since we cannot generate new names in HOcore, free
variables
f
are also numbers, and we use the same translation for
them. We also use non-deterministic internal choice, encoded as
follows:

P+Q\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\smalltriangleup}}{{=}}\overline{ch}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}\parallel\overline{ch}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}\parallel ch(x).ch(\_).x
: both messages are consumed, and only one process
is executed. This encoding supposes that at most one choice is active
at a given time, as we use only one name
ch
to encode all the
choices. We also use
n
-ary choices for
n>2
in
Section
5.3
, which can be encoded in the same way.

A stack is represented as in the KAM, by messages on
hd_{\mathsf{c}}
and
c
, and the
translation of an application
\left\ldbrack t\;s\right\rdbrack
is exactly the same as for the
KAM. The encoding of the empty context
[]
is different, however, because
contexts are used to distinguish between execution paths at two points in the
machine: when evaluating a function
\lambda x.t
in evaluation mode, and when
deciding whether the execution is finished in continuation mode. The empty context is
thus encoded as
b(x).x
, waiting to receive the process to execute in the
empty case. For the non-empty case, this input on
b
is absent and there are
instead messages on
hd_{\mathsf{c}}
and
c
. Thus the generic way to choose a branch is as
follows:

\overline{b}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}\parallel hd_{\mathsf{c}}(x).c(y).b(\_).\text{do this if non-empty}.

In the non-empty case, the input on
b
discards the message for the empty
behavior that was not used.

For
\lambda
-abstractions, the behavior for the empty case is described in the
process
\mathsf{Restart}
. More precisely,
\left\ldbrack\lambda x.t\right\rdbrack
receives the current stack

\left\ldbrack\pi\right\rdbrack
on
c
to run it in parallel with

\overline{b}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}\parallel hd_{\mathsf{c}}(x).b(\_).\left\ldbrack t\right\rdbrack
. If
\left\ldbrack\pi\right\rdbrack

is of the form
\overline{hd_{\mathsf{c}}}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}\parallel{\overline{c}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}}
, then we
have the same behavior as with the KAM, with an extra
communication on
b
to garbage collect the
\mathsf{Restart}
process. Otherwise,

\left\ldbrack\pi\right\rdbrack=b(x).x
and we obtain the following sequence of transitions.

\displaystyle b(x).x\parallel\overline{b}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}\parallel hd_{\mathsf{c}}(x).b(\_).\left\ldbrack t\right\rdbrack\parallel\overline{k}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\;

\displaystyle\ocirc(\_).k(x).{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mskip 0.0mu plus 8.0mu minus 1.0mu\mskip 0.0mu plus 8.0mu minus 1.0mu\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right)$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mskip 0.0mu plus 8.0mu minus 1.0mu\mskip 0.0mu plus 8.0mu minus 1.0mu\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right)$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mskip 0.0mu plus 8.0mu minus 1.0mu\mskip 0.0mu plus 8.0mu minus 1.0mu\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right)$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mskip 0.0mu plus 8.0mu minus 1.0mu\mskip 0.0mu plus 8.0mu minus 1.0mu\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right)$}}}}}}\parallel hd_{\mathsf{c}}(x).b(\_).\left\ldbrack t\right\rdbrack\parallel\overline{k}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\ocirc$}}}\;

\displaystyle\overline{hd_{\mathsf{c}}}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}\parallel\overline{k}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}\parallel\overline{c}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\c \displaystyle\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\;

\displaystyle\overline{k}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}\parallel\overline{c}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}\parallel\overline{b}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\ran b(\_).\left\ldbrack[n\mathbin{/}x]t\right\rdbrack

\displaystyle\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\;

\displaystyle\overline{k}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}\parallel\overline{c}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}\parallel\left\ldbrack[n\mathbin{/}x]t\right\rdbrack

In the end, we have effectively restarted the machine to evaluate
[n\mathbin{/}x]t
, as wished.

In continuation mode, the branching is done by the process
\mathsf{Cont}
,
which is executed after applying the transition
Var
. More
precisely, a free variable
f
is translated using
\left\ldbrack.\right\rdbrack_{\mathsf{Int}}
,
which signals first the value of
f
(with the names
suc

and
z
), and then sends a message on
init
to enter the continuation
mode. The way the NFB machine chooses which
t_{i}
to evaluate in a
stack
t_{1}\mathop{::}\ldots\mathop{::}t_{m}\mathop{::}[]
is a recursive
mechanism, and recursion can be encoded in a higher-order calculus:

\mathsf{Rec}\parallel\overline{rec}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}
reduces to

\mathsf{Cont}\parallel\mathsf{Rec}\parallel\overline{rec}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}
when it receives a
message on
init
. The process
\mathsf{Cont}
is doing a case analysis on

\left\ldbrack\pi\right\rdbrack
when it is executed in parallel with
\overline{c}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}
: if

\pi=[]
, then
\left\ldbrack\pi\right\rdbrack=b(x).x
receives the message on

b
which flags
\medstar
and the machine stops. Otherwise,

\left\ldbrack\pi\right\rdbrack=\overline{hd_{\mathsf{c}}}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}\parallel\overline{c}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}
, and we
have the following reductions:

\displaystyle\mathsf{Cont}\parallel\overline{c}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\overline{hd_{\mathsf{c}}}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}\parallel\overline{c}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}\parallel\overline{b}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}\parallel hd_{\mathsf{c}}(x).b(\_).\mathsf{Chce}(x)

\displaystyle\mathbin{{\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}}^{2}}\overline{c}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}\parallel\mathsf{Chce}(\left\ldbrack t\right\rdbrack)

At this point,
\mathsf{Chce}(\left\ldbrack t\right\rdbrack)
either evaluates
t
with flag
\filledmedtriangledown
, or
flags
\filledmedtriangleright
and continues exploring
\pi^{\prime}
. In the former case, the current
stack
\pi^{\prime}
is replaced by an empty stack, and in the latter, a message on

init
is issued to produce
\mathsf{Cont}\parallel\overline{c}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}
after some
reduction steps.

4.4.
Operational Correspondence and Full Abstraction

Establishing full abstraction requires first to state the
correspondence between the NFB machine and its translation. We call

machine process
a process obtained by reducing a translated
configuration.

{defi}

A process
P
is a machine process if there exists a configuration
C
of
the machine such that
\left\ldbrack C\right\rdbrack\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}P
.

We recall that
F
range over the flags of the NFB machine, i.e.,

\ocirc
,
\filledmedtriangledown
,
\filledmedtriangleright
,
\medstar
, and
f
. We let

\hat{F}
range over flags and
\tau
. The translation maps the flags
to corresponding channel names, except for
f
, which is
represented using the
suc
and
z
channel names. We call
flag
names
the channel names
\ocirc
,
\filledmedtriangledown
,
\filledmedtriangleright
,
\medstar
,

suc
, and
z
. We write
\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$f$}}}
as a shorthand for
f

transitions
\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$suc$}}}
followed by a transition
\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$z$}}}
, i.e.,

(\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$suc$}}})^{f}\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$z$}}}
, and
\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$f$}}}
stands for

\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$f$}}}\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}
. We define
\mathbb{H}
as the set
of names in the translation that are not flag names. We write

\downarrow_{\gamma}
as a shorthand for
\downarrow_{\gamma}^{\mathbb{H}}
,

\Downarrow_{\gamma}
for
\Downarrow_{\gamma}^{\mathbb{H}}
,
\mathsf{WkObs}(P)
for

\mathsf{WkObs}_{\mathbb{H}}(P)
, and
\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle{HO}}}}
for
\mathrel{\approx^{\mathbb{H}}_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle{HO}}}}
.

Given a machine process
P
and a flag
\hat{F}
, we define a set

\mathsf{next}(\hat{F},P)
such that
Q\in\mathsf{next}(\hat{F},P)
implies there is
a weak reduction between
P
and
Q
with at least one transition
labeled
\hat{F}
,
Q
is the translation of a configuration, and
there is no other machine translation in between. Intuitively, it is
the set of translations that are reachable from
P
just after a step

\hat{F}
.

{defi}

We define
\mathsf{next}(\hat{F},P)
so that
Q\in\mathsf{next}(\hat{F},P)

implies
P\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\hat{F}$}}}\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}Q
,

Q=\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}\right\rdbrack
for some
C^{\prime}
, and for any
P^{\prime}
such that

P^{\prime}\neq P
,
P^{\prime}\neq Q
, and
P\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\hat{F}$}}}P^{\prime}\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}Q

or
P\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}P^{\prime}\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\hat{F}$}}}Q
, we have

P^{\prime}\neq\left\ldbrack C\right\rdbrack
for any
C
.

The translation has been designed so that each machine process is
either stuck waiting for a message on a flag name, or has at most one
possible communication, except when a non-deterministic choice has to
be made. The latter case occurs when the NFB machine is exploring a
stack, with a translated configuration of the form

\left\ldbrack\langle t\mathop{::}\pi,n\rangle_{\mathsf{ct}}\right\rdbrack
: as explained at the end of
Section
4.3
, we have

\left\ldbrack\langle t\mathop{::}\pi,n\rangle_{\mathsf{ct}}\right\rdbrack\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\mathsf{Chce}(\left\ldbrack t\right\rdbrack)\parallel\overline{c}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}\parallel\overline{k}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}\parallel\mathsf{Rec}\parallel\overline{rec}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}

We call the resulting process

\mathsf{Choice}(t,\pi,n)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\smalltriangleup}}{{=}}\mathsf{Chce}(\left\ldbrack t\right\rdbrack)\parallel\overline{c}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}\parallel\overline{k}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}\parallel\mathsf{Rec}\parallel\overline{rec}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}
a
choice process
, as it is about to make an arbitrary
choice in
\mathsf{Chce}(\left\ldbrack t\right\rdbrack)
. Unfolding the encoding of choice, we have

\mathsf{Chce}(\left\ldbrack t\right\rdbrack)=\overline{ch}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}\parallel\overline{ch}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}\parallel ch(x).ch(\_).x
, with

P_{\filledmedtriangledown,t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\smalltriangleup}}{{=}}\filledmedtriangledown(\_).c(\_).{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left(\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mskip 0.0mu plus 8.0mu minus 1.0mu\mskip 0.0mu plus 8.0mu minus 1.0mu\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right)$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left(\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mskip 0.0mu plus 8.0mu minus 1.0mu\mskip 0.0mu plus 8.0mu minus 1.0mu\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right)$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left(\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mskip 0.0mu plus 8.0mu minus 1.0mu\mskip 0.0mu plus 8.0mu minus 1.0mu\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right)$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left(\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mskip 0.0mu plus 8.0mu minus 1.0mu\mskip 0.0mu plus 8.0mu minus 1.0mu\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right)$}}}}}}
and

P_{\filledmedtriangleright}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\smalltriangleup}}{{=}}\filledmedtriangleright(\_).\overline{init}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}
. Let

P_{\pi,n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\smalltriangleup}}{{=}}\overline{c}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}\parallel\overline{k}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}\parallel\mathsf{Rec}\parallel\overline{rec}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}
. Then the two possible
transitions from
\mathsf{Choice}(t,\pi,n)
lead to the following machine
translations:

\displaystyle\mathsf{Choice}(t,\pi,n)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\overline{ch}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}\parallel ch(\_).P_{\filledmedtriangledown,t}\parallel P_{\pi,n}\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}P_{\filledmedtriangledown,t}\parallel P_{\pi,n}\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\filledmedtriangledown$}}}{\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}}^{2}\left\ldbrack\langle t,[],n\rangle_{\mathsf{ev}}\right\rdbrack

\displaystyle\mathsf{Choice}(t,\pi,n)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\overline{ch}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}\parallel ch(\_).P_{\filledmedtriangleright}\parallel P_{\pi,n}\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}P_{\filledmedtriangleright}\parallel P_{\pi,n}\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\filledmedtriangleright$}}}{\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}}^{2}\left\ldbrack\langle\pi,n\rangle_{\mathsf{ct}}\right\rdbrack

The first transition
\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}
is making the choice. From there,
one process flags
\filledmedtriangledown
, while the other flags
\filledmedtriangleright
: we know
which choice has been made by looking at the weak observable
actions. Note that
\mathsf{next}(\tau,\mathsf{Choice}(t,\pi,n))
is empty,
because we need to flag either
\filledmedtriangledown
or
\filledmedtriangleright
to reach a
machine translation.

The next lemma expresses the fact that the translation is
deterministic, relying on flags in the case of choice processes.

Lemma 6

(Determinism)
.

Let
P
be a machine process.

•

If
P
is not a choice process, then either
P
cannot reduce,
or there exist a unique
P^{\prime}
and
\hat{F}
such that

P\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\hat{F}$}}}P^{\prime}
;

•

if
P
is a choice process, then there exist
P_{1}
and
P_{2}

such that
P\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}P_{1}
,
P\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}P_{2}
, and

\mathsf{WkObs}(P_{1})\neq\mathsf{WkObs}(P_{2})
. For all
\hat{F}
,
P^{\prime}
such that

P\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\hat{F}$}}}P^{\prime}
, we have
\hat{F}=\tau
and either
P^{\prime}=P_{1}
or
P^{\prime}=P_{2}
.

Proof 4.2
.

The proof is a straightforward case analysis on the shape of the
machine processes, checking that for each of them, the lemma holds.

Lemma
6
implies that
\mathsf{next}(\hat{F},P)
is either
empty or a singleton for every
\hat{F}
and
P
. In the following, we
write
\mathsf{next}(\hat{F},P)
to assert that it is not empty and to
directly denote the corresponding unique machine translation.

A consequence is that a machine process may only have flag names as
possible observable actions. In particular, it cannot have a coname

\overline{a}
as an observable action.

Corollary 7
.

For all machine process
P
,

\mathsf{WkObs}(P)\subseteq\{\ocirc,\filledmedtriangledown,\filledmedtriangleright,\medstar,suc,z\}
.

The second property we need is an operational correspondence between
the machine and its translation.

Lemma 8

(Faithfulness)
.

The following holds:

•

C\mathrel{\xmapsto{\makebox[5.19876pt]{\scriptsize$\hat{F}$}}}C^{\prime}
iff

\mathsf{next}(\hat{F},\left\ldbrack C\right\rdbrack)=\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}\right\rdbrack
;

•

C\mathrel{\xmapsto{\makebox[5.19876pt]{\scriptsize$\medstar$}}}
iff
\left\ldbrack C\right\rdbrack\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\medstar$}}}P

and
P\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle{HO}}}}{\boldsymbol{0}}
.

Proof 4.3
.

The proof is by a straightforward case analysis on
C
in the “only
if” direction, and by case analysis on
\left\ldbrack C\right\rdbrack
in the “if”
direction.

With these properties, we can prove that the translation from NFB
machines to HOcore processes is fully abstract. We define the process
complementing a flag as follows: for a flag
F\neq f
, we
write
\overline{F}
for the process
\overline{F}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}
. We define

\overline{f}
inductively so that

\overline{n+1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\smalltriangleup}}{{=}}\overline{suc}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}\parallel\overline{n}
and

\overline{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\smalltriangleup}}{{=}}\overline{z}{{\mathchoice{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\textstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}{{}\mathopen{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\langle\vphantom{\cdot}\hskip-1.2pt\right.$}}}}\mathclose{{{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left.\hskip-1.2pt\vphantom{\cdot}\right\rangle$}}}}}}}
. The following lemma allows us to
reason up to
\tau
-transitions on the HOcore side.

Lemma 9
.

Let
P
,
P^{\prime}
be machine processes. If
P\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}P^{\prime}
and

\mathsf{WkObs}(P)=\mathsf{WkObs}(P^{\prime})
, then
P\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle{HO}}}}P^{\prime}
.

Proof 4.4
.

One can check that

\{(P\parallel R,P^{\prime}\parallel R)\;\;\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}\;\;P\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}P^{\prime},\mathsf{WkObs}(P)=\mathsf{WkObs}(P^{\prime})\}\cup\{(R,R)\}
is a barbed bisimulation using
Lemma
6
.

Lemma 10
.

If
\left\ldbrack C\right\rdbrack\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle{HO}}}}\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}\right\rdbrack
, then
C\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{m}}}C^{\prime}
.

Proof 4.5
.

We prove that

\mathord{\mathrel{\mathcal{R}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\smalltriangleup}}{{=}}\{(C,C^{\prime})\;\;\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}\;\;\left\ldbrack C\right\rdbrack\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle{HO}}}}\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}\right\rdbrack\}
is a machine bisimulation. Let
C_{1}\mathrel{\mathcal{R}}C^{\prime}_{1}
.

Suppose
C_{1}\mathrel{{\mathrel{\xmapsto{\makebox[5.19876pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}}^{*}}C_{2}\mathrel{\xmapsto{\makebox[5.19876pt]{\scriptsize$F$}}}C_{3}
. By
Lemma
8
, we have

\left\ldbrack C_{1}\right\rdbrack\parallel\overline{F}\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\left\ldbrack C_{2}\right\rdbrack\parallel\overline{F}\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\left\ldbrack C_{3}\right\rdbrack.

Because
\left\ldbrack C_{1}\right\rdbrack\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle{HO}}}}\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}_{1}\right\rdbrack
, there exists
P
such
that
\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}_{1}\right\rdbrack\parallel\overline{F}\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}P
and

\left\ldbrack C_{3}\right\rdbrack\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle{HO}}}}P
. Since
\mathsf{WkObs}(\left\ldbrack C_{3}\right\rdbrack)=\mathsf{WkObs}(P)
, we
have in particular
\neg{(P\downarrow_{\overline{F}})}
by
Corollary
7
. Consequently, there exists
P^{\prime}
such that

\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}_{1}\right\rdbrack\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}P^{\prime}
,
P^{\prime}\downarrow_{F}
, and

P^{\prime}\parallel\overline{F}\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}P
. Consider

\mathsf{next}(F,P^{\prime})=\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}_{3}\right\rdbrack
for some
C^{\prime}_{3}
, which must exists since

P^{\prime}
still has a barb on
F
. We have

\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}_{1}\right\rdbrack\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}P^{\prime}\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$F$}}}\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}_{3}\right\rdbrack
, which
implies
C^{\prime}_{1}\mathrel{{\mathrel{\xmapsto{\makebox[5.19876pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}}^{*}}\mathrel{\xmapsto{\makebox[5.19876pt]{\scriptsize$F$}}}C^{\prime}_{3}
by
Lemma
8
. To show that
C_{3}\mathrel{\mathcal{R}}C^{\prime}_{3}
, we
need to prove that
\left\ldbrack C_{3}\right\rdbrack\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle{HO}}}}\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}_{3}\right\rdbrack
. Because

\left\ldbrack C_{3}\right\rdbrack\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle{HO}}}}P
, it is enough to prove

P\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle{HO}}}}\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}_{3}\right\rdbrack
.

Suppose there is a choice process
Q
so that

P^{\prime}\parallel\overline{F}\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}Q\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}_{3}\right\rdbrack
. It means that a communication on

F^{\prime}\in\{\filledmedtriangledown,\filledmedtriangleright\}
happens, as it is the only
possibility for a choice process to reach a translated
configuration. The communication cannot be with
\overline{F}
,
because that output is consumed by
P^{\prime}
. Therefore, it implies that

P^{\prime}\Downarrow_{\overline{F^{\prime}}}
, but
P^{\prime}
is a machine process, so it is
not possible by Corollary
7
.

In the end, we have both
P^{\prime}\parallel\overline{F}\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}P

and
P^{\prime}\parallel\overline{F}\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}_{3}\right\rdbrack
, and there
is no choice process between
P^{\prime}\parallel\overline{F}
and

\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}_{3}\right\rdbrack
. As this is the only source of non-determinism, by
Lemma
6
, we either have
P\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}_{3}\right\rdbrack
or
\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}_{3}\right\rdbrack\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}P
.

We now prove that
\mathsf{WkObs}(\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}_{3}\right\rdbrack)=\mathsf{WkObs}(P)
, to be able to
conclude with Lemma
9
. The only way to have

\mathsf{WkObs}(\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}_{3}\right\rdbrack)\neq\mathsf{WkObs}(P)
is if a choice has been made
between
\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}_{3}\right\rdbrack
and
P
, i.e., if there is a choice process
Q

making at least one
\tau
-transition between the two. The case

P\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}Q\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}_{3}\right\rdbrack
has been
ruled out, because there is no choice process between

P^{\prime}\parallel\overline{F}
and
\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}_{3}\right\rdbrack
. The other possibility
is
\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}_{3}\right\rdbrack\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}Q\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}P
, where
Q
is a
choice process. Because a
choice has been made, we have either

\mathsf{WkObs}(P)\subseteq\{\filledmedtriangledown\}
or

\mathsf{WkObs}(P)\subseteq\{\filledmedtriangleright\}
. However, we have

\mathsf{WkObs}(P)=\mathsf{WkObs}(\left\ldbrack C_{3}\right\rdbrack)
, and the translation of a
configuration cannot have only a single flag of a non-deterministic
choice as weak observable action, which leads to a
contradiction. Consequently, we have

\mathsf{WkObs}(P)=\mathsf{WkObs}(\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}_{3}\right\rdbrack)
, hence

P\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle{HO}}}}\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}_{3}\right\rdbrack
by Lemma
9
.

Suppose
C_{1}\mathrel{{\mathrel{\xmapsto{\makebox[5.19876pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}}^{*}}C_{2}\mathrel{\xmapsto{\makebox[5.19876pt]{\scriptsize$\medstar$}}}
; then

\left\ldbrack C_{1}\right\rdbrack\parallel\overline{\medstar}\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\left\ldbrack C_{2}\right\rdbrack\parallel\overline{\medstar}\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}P
with
P\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle{HO}}}}{\boldsymbol{0}}
by
Lemma
8
. Therefore, there exists
P^{\prime}
such that

\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}_{1}\right\rdbrack\parallel\overline{\medstar}\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}P^{\prime}
and

P\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle{HO}}}}P^{\prime}\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle{HO}}}}{\boldsymbol{0}}
. With Lemma
8
, we
have
C^{\prime}_{1}\mathrel{{\mathrel{\xmapsto{\makebox[5.19876pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}}^{*}}\mathrel{\xmapsto{\makebox[5.19876pt]{\scriptsize$\medstar$}}}
, as wished.

Lemma 11
.

If
C\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{m}}}C^{\prime}
, then
\left\ldbrack C\right\rdbrack\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle{HO}}}}\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}\right\rdbrack
.

Proof 4.6
.

We prove that

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathcal{R}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\smalltriangleup}}{{=}}\left\{\begin{gathered}(P\parallel R,Q\parallel R)\;\;\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}\;\;C\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{m}}}C^{\prime},\mathsf{WkObs}(P)=\mathsf{WkObs}(Q),\mathsf{fn}(R)\cap\mathbb{H}=\emptyset\\
P\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\left\ldbrack C\right\rdbrack,Q\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}\right\rdbrack\mbox{ or
}\left\ldbrack C\right\rdbrack\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}P,\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}\right\rdbrack\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}Q\\
\end{gathered}\right\}

\displaystyle\cup\{(P\parallel R,Q\parallel R)\;\;\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}\;\;P\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle{HO}}}}{\boldsymbol{0}}\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle{HO}}}}Q,\mathsf{fn}(R)\cap\mathbb{H}=\emptyset\}

is a barbed bisimulation. The congruence condition holds by
definition of
\mathrel{\mathcal{R}}
, and the observable actions are the same
because of the condition
\mathsf{WkObs}(P)=\mathsf{WkObs}(Q)
. The
reductions from
R
are easy to match; we thus only check the
reductions involving
P
or
Q
.

If
P\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}P^{\prime}
, then we show that there exists
Q^{\prime}
such that

Q\parallel R\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}Q^{\prime}\parallel R
and

P^{\prime}\parallel R\mathrel{\mathcal{R}}Q^{\prime}\parallel R
. If
P\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\left\ldbrack C\right\rdbrack

and
Q\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}\right\rdbrack
, then taking
Q^{\prime}=\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}\right\rdbrack

works. If
\left\ldbrack C\right\rdbrack\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}P
and
\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}\right\rdbrack\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}Q
,
then we distinguish two cases. If
P
is not a choice process, then

\mathsf{WkObs}(P)=\mathsf{WkObs}(P^{\prime})
, and we can simply choose
Q^{\prime}=Q
. Otherwise, the transition
P\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}P^{\prime}
is making a choice
and
\mathsf{WkObs}(P^{\prime})\subsetneq\mathsf{WkObs}(P)
. But
\mathsf{WkObs}(P)=\mathsf{WkObs}(Q)

implies that
Q
also reduces to a choice process. Then it is
possible to choose the same branch as
P^{\prime}
and to define
Q^{\prime}

accordingly.

Suppose
P\parallel R\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}P^{\prime}
with a communication on a
flag
F
—a communication on another name is not possible
because
\mathsf{fn}(R)\cap\mathbb{H}=\emptyset
. Then
R\downarrow_{\overline{F}}
and
P\downarrow_{F}
, which is possible only in the case

\left\ldbrack C\right\rdbrack\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}P
. Therefore we also have
\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}\right\rdbrack\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}Q
by definition of
\mathrel{\mathcal{R}}
.

Suppose
F\neq\medstar
: then we also have

P\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$F$}}}P^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\left\ldbrack C^{\prime\prime}\right\rdbrack
for some
P^{\prime\prime}
with

\left\ldbrack C^{\prime\prime}\right\rdbrack=\mathsf{next}(F,P)
. By Lemma
6
,
there exist
R^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}=P^{\prime\prime}\parallel R^{\prime}
. We have

\left\ldbrack C\right\rdbrack\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$F$}}}\left\ldbrack C^{\prime\prime}\right\rdbrack
, which implies

C\mathrel{{\mathrel{\xmapsto{\makebox[5.19876pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}}^{*}}\mathrel{\xmapsto{\makebox[5.19876pt]{\scriptsize$F$}}}C^{\prime\prime}
by
Lemma
8
. Because
C\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{m}}}C^{\prime}
, there
exists
C^{\prime\prime\prime}
such that

C^{\prime}\mathrel{{\mathrel{\xmapsto{\makebox[5.19876pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}}^{*}}\mathrel{\xmapsto{\makebox[5.19876pt]{\scriptsize$F$}}}C^{\prime\prime\prime}
and

C^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{m}}}C^{\prime\prime\prime}
. By Lemma
8
, this implies

\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}\right\rdbrack\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$F$}}}\left\ldbrack C^{\prime\prime\prime}\right\rdbrack
; in particular, there exists

Q^{\prime\prime}
such that

\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}\right\rdbrack\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$F$}}}Q^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\left\ldbrack C^{\prime\prime\prime}\right\rdbrack
. By Lemma
6
, we also have

Q\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$F$}}}Q^{\prime\prime}
, therefore

Q\parallel R\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}Q^{\prime\prime}\parallel R^{\prime}
. To show that

P^{\prime\prime}\parallel R^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathcal{R}}Q^{\prime\prime}\parallel R^{\prime}
, what is left to check is

\mathsf{WkObs}(P^{\prime\prime})=\mathsf{WkObs}(Q^{\prime\prime})
. We have
P^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\left\ldbrack C^{\prime\prime}\right\rdbrack
,
so there is no choice process between
P^{\prime\prime}
and
\left\ldbrack C^{\prime\prime}\right\rdbrack
,
because a transition with label
\filledmedtriangledown
or
\filledmedtriangleright
is necessary
to go from a choice process to the translation of a
configuration. Therefore, we have
\mathsf{WkObs}(P^{\prime\prime})=\mathsf{WkObs}(\left\ldbrack C^{\prime\prime}\right\rdbrack)
, and similarly
\mathsf{WkObs}(Q^{\prime\prime})=\mathsf{WkObs}(\left\ldbrack C^{\prime\prime\prime}\right\rdbrack)
. Because
C^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{m}}}C^{\prime\prime\prime}
, any action
from
C^{\prime\prime}
is matched by
C^{\prime\prime\prime}
and conversely, which implies

\mathsf{WkObs}(\left\ldbrack C^{\prime\prime}\right\rdbrack)=\mathsf{WkObs}(\left\ldbrack C^{\prime\prime\prime}\right\rdbrack)
by
Lemma
8
. In the end, we have
\mathsf{WkObs}(P^{\prime\prime})=\mathsf{WkObs}(Q^{\prime\prime})
, and therefore
P^{\prime\prime}\parallel R^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathcal{R}}Q^{\prime\prime}\parallel R^{\prime}
, as wished.

If
F=\medstar
, then
P\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\medstar$}}}P^{\prime\prime}
for some
P^{\prime\prime}
such
that
P^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle{HO}}}}{\boldsymbol{0}}
. By Lemma
6
, there
exists
R^{\prime}
such that
P^{\prime}=P^{\prime\prime}\parallel R^{\prime}
. We have

\left\ldbrack C\right\rdbrack\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\medstar$}}}P^{\prime\prime}
, so

C\mathrel{{\mathrel{\xmapsto{\makebox[5.19876pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}}^{*}}\mathrel{\xmapsto{\makebox[5.19876pt]{\scriptsize$\medstar$}}}
holds by
Lemma
8
. Because
C\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{m}}}C^{\prime}
, we have

C^{\prime}\mathrel{{\mathrel{\xmapsto{\makebox[5.19876pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}}^{*}}\mathrel{\xmapsto{\makebox[5.19876pt]{\scriptsize$\medstar$}}}
, so by Lemma
8
,
there exists
Q^{\prime\prime}
such that

\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}\right\rdbrack\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\medstar$}}}Q^{\prime\prime}
and
Q^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle{HO}}}}{\boldsymbol{0}}
. By Lemma
6
, we also have

Q\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\medstar$}}}Q^{\prime\prime}
, therefore we have

Q\parallel R\mathrel{\xRightarrow{\makebox[54.40016pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}Q^{\prime\prime}\parallel R^{\prime}
. The resulting
processes
P^{\prime\prime}\parallel R^{\prime}
and
Q^{\prime\prime}\parallel R^{\prime}
are in the
second set defining
\mathrel{\mathcal{R}}
.

Theorem 12
.

C\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{m}}}C^{\prime}
iff
\left\ldbrack C\right\rdbrack\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle{HO}}}}\left\ldbrack C^{\prime}\right\rdbrack
.

Proof 4.7
.

By Lemmas
10
and
11
.

As a result, we can deduce full abstraction between HOcore and the

\lambda
-calculus with normal-form bisimilarity.

Corollary 13
.

t\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{nf}}}s
iff there exists
n>\max{(\mathsf{fv}(t)\cup\mathsf{fv}(s))}
such that

\left\ldbrack\langle t,[],n\rangle_{\mathsf{ev}}\right\rdbrack\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle{HO}}}}\left\ldbrack\langle s,[],n\rangle_{\mathsf{ev}}\right\rdbrack
.

Proof 4.8
.

By Theorems
5
and
12
.

The proofs of Lemmas
10
and
11
rely
on the translation being deterministic (Lemma
6
) and
faithful (Lemma
8
). In particular, flagged
non-determinism in machines is represented by choice processes
in HOcore, so that these processes make the choice observable with different
flags before reducing to the next translated configurations. The
machine translations defined in the upcoming sections satisfy these
properties, and the correspondence between machines and their
translation in HOcore can be proved in a similar fashion.

5.
Applicative Bisimilarity

Proving full abstraction w.r.t. normal-form bisimilarity requires
minimal interactions—synchronizations on flags—between a
machine process and the outside. Achieving full abstraction w.r.t. applicative bisimilarity is intuitively more difficult, since this
bisimilarity tests
\lambda
-abstractions by applying them to an
arbitrary argument. Internalizing such bisimilarity is simple using
higher-order flags: one may think of the following transition
to test the result of a computation:

\langle\lambda x.t,[]\rangle\mathrel{\xmapsto{\makebox[5.19876pt]{\scriptsize$s$}}}\langle[s\mathbin{/}x]t,[]\rangle

Although HOcore has higher-order communications, we cannot use them
to obtain a fully abstract encoding of such a machine for two
reasons. First, allowing interactions where the environment provides a
term may allow arbitrary processes to be received, including processes
that are not in the image of the translation, thus potentially
breaking invariants of the translation. Second, the translation of the
KAM has to hide the names it uses for the translation to be fully
abstract; it is thus impossible for the context to use such names and
to provide translated
\lambda
-terms to be tested.

We thus propose in this section to internalize applicative
bisimilarity using ordinary flags: when the abstract machine reaches a
value, it switches to a different mode where it non-deterministically
builds a test term step by step, using flags to indicate its choices
so as to ensure that a bisimilar machine builds the same term. The
translation of such a machine into HOcore is then similar to the
translation of the NFB machine.

Using simple flags to generate terms step by step implies we need to
deal with binders. In particular, and anticipating on the HOcore translation, we no longer can rely on the definition of binding and
substitution from HOcore, as we cannot write a process that inputs a
translation of
t
and outputs a translation of
\lambda x.t
using an
HOcore binding for
x
. We thus switch to a pure data description of
bindings, using de Bruijn indices. As such terms still need to be
executed, we first recall the definition of the KAM with de Bruijn
indices and the definitions of contextual equivalence and applicative
bisimilarity for
\lambda
-terms with de Bruijn indices. We then
present the machine internalizing applicative bisimilarity, its
translation into HOcore, and show they are fully abstract. We finally
conclude this section by showing how contextual equivalence is
internalized in an abstract machine, generating contexts instead of
terms.

5.1.
The KAM and Behavioral Equivalences

In the
\lambda
-calculus with de Bruijn indices, a variable is a number that
indicates how many
\lambda
’s are between the variable and its binder. For
example,
\lambda x.x
is written
\lambda.0
and
\lambda xy.x\;y
is written

\lambda.\lambda.1\;0
. The syntax of terms (
t,s
), closures (
\eta
),
environments (
e,d
), and values (
v
) is as follows.

t,s\Coloneqq n\;\;\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}\;\;t\;s\;\;\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}\;\;\lambda.t\qquad\eta\Coloneqq(t,e)\qquad e,d\Coloneqq\eta\mathop{::}e\;\;\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}\;\;\epsilon\qquad v\Coloneqq(\lambda.t,e)\\

A
closure

\eta
is a pair
(t,e)
where
e
is an
environment mapping the free variables of
t
to closures;
environments are used in lieu of substitutions. A term
t
is closed
if
\mathsf{fv}(t)=\emptyset
, and a closure
(t,e)
is closed if the
number of elements of
e
is bigger than the highest free variable
of
t
, and
e
is composed only of closed closures.

The semantics is given by the original, environment-based KAM, where a
configuration
C
is now composed of the closed closure
(t,e)
being
evaluated, and a stack
\pi
of closures. The transitions rules are
as follows.

C\Coloneqq\langle t,e,\pi\rangle_{\mathcal{}\mathsf{ev}}\mbox{ (configurations)}\qquad\pi\Coloneqq\eta\mathop{::}\pi\;\;\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}\;\;[]\mbox{ (stacks)}

\displaystyle\langle t\;s,e,\pi\rangle_{\mathcal{}\mathsf{ev}}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\xmapsto{\makebox[12.51709pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\langle t,e,(s,e)\mathop{::}\pi\rangle_{\mathcal{}\mathsf{ev}}

(
Push
)

\displaystyle\langle 0,(t,e)\mathop{::}d,\pi\rangle_{\mathcal{}\mathsf{ev}}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\xmapsto{\makebox[12.51709pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\langle t,e,\pi\rangle_{\mathcal{}\mathsf{ev}}

(
Zero
)

\displaystyle\langle n+1,(t,e)\mathop{::}d,\pi\rangle_{\mathcal{}\mathsf{ev}}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\xmapsto{\makebox[12.51709pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\langle n,d,\pi\rangle_{\mathcal{}\mathsf{ev}}

(
Env
)

\displaystyle\langle\lambda.t,e,\eta\mathop{::}\pi\rangle_{\mathcal{}\mathsf{ev}}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\xmapsto{\makebox[12.51709pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}\langle t,\eta\mathop{::}e,\pi\rangle_{\mathcal{}\mathsf{ev}}

(
Grab
)

In
Push
, the argument
s
of an application is
stored on the stack with its environment
e
while the term
t
in function
position is evaluated. If we get a
\lambda
-abstraction
\lambda.t
(transition

Grab
), then an argument
\eta
is moved from the stack to the top of
the environment to remember that
\eta
corresponds to the de Bruijn index 0,
and the evaluation continues with
t
. Looking up the closure corresponding to a
de Bruijn index in the environment is done with the rules
Env
and

Zero
. Because we evaluate closed closures only, it is not possible to
obtain a configuration of the form
\langle n,\epsilon,\pi\rangle_{\mathcal{}\mathsf{ev}}
. If a configuration of
the form
\langle\lambda.t,e,[]\rangle_{\mathcal{}\mathsf{ev}}
is reached, then the evaluation is finished,
and the result is
(\lambda.t,e)
.

Behavioral equivalences

Contextual equivalence compares closed
terms by testing them within all contexts. A context
\mathcal{C}
is a term with a
hole
\square
at a variable position; plugging a term
t
in
\mathcal{C}
is written

\mathcal{C}[t]
. A context is closed if
\mathsf{fv}(\mathcal{C})=\emptyset
. Contextual
equivalence is then defined as follows.

{defi}

Two closed terms
t
and
s
are contextually equivalent, written

t\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{ctx}}}s
, if for all closed contexts
\mathcal{C}
,

\langle\mathcal{C}[t],\epsilon,[]\rangle_{\mathcal{}\mathsf{ev}}\mathrel{{\mathbin{\xmapsto{\makebox[12.51709pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}}^{*}}\langle\lambda.t^{\prime},e,[]\rangle_{\mathcal{}\mathsf{ev}}
for some
t^{\prime}
and
e
iff

\langle\mathcal{C}[s],\epsilon,[]\rangle_{\mathcal{}\mathsf{ev}}\mathrel{{\mathbin{\xmapsto{\makebox[12.51709pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}}^{*}}\langle\lambda.s^{\prime},d,[]\rangle_{\mathcal{}\mathsf{ev}}
for some
s^{\prime}
and
d
.

Contextual equivalence is characterized by applicative
bisimilarity
[
AO93
]
, which reduces closed terms to values
that are then applied to an arbitrary argument.

{defi}

A symmetric relation
\mathrel{\mathcal{R}}
on closed closures is an applicative bisimulation
if
(t,e)\mathrel{\mathcal{R}}(s,d)
and

\langle t,e,[]\rangle_{\mathcal{}\mathsf{ev}}\mathrel{{\mathbin{\xmapsto{\makebox[12.51709pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}}^{*}}\langle\lambda.t^{\prime},e^{\prime},[]\rangle_{\mathcal{}\mathsf{ev}}
implies that
there exist
s^{\prime}
and
d^{\prime}
such that

\langle s,d,[]\rangle_{\mathcal{}\mathsf{ev}}\mathrel{{\mathbin{\xmapsto{\makebox[12.51709pt]{\scriptsize$\tau$}}}}^{*}}\langle\lambda.s^{\prime},d^{\prime},[]\rangle_{\mathcal{}\mathsf{ev}}
, and for all
closed
t^{\prime\prime}
, we have

(t^{\prime},(t^{\prime\prime},\epsilon)\mathop{::}e^{\prime})\mathrel{\mathcal{R}}(s^{\prime},(t^{\prime\prime},\epsilon)\mathop{::}d^{\prime})
.

Applicative bisimilarity
\mathrel{\approx_{\mathsf{app}}}
is the largest applicative
bisimulation.

We can prove full abstraction between HOcore and the
\lambda
-calculus by
either internalizing contextual equivalence or applicative bisimilarity. We
choose the latter, as it is closer to normal-form bisimilarity. We briefly show
in Section
5.4
how the machine of Section
5.2

can be adapted to handle contextual equivalence.

5.2.
Argument Generation for the Applicative Bisimilarity

After evaluating a term thanks to the KAM, we want to produce a closed
argument to pass it to the resulting value, and then restart the
evaluation process. The approach of
[
CF11
]
consists in
waiting for a name from the outside, giving access to a translated

\lambda
-term to be applied to. If the environment does not provide
access to a well-formed translation, the process simulating

\beta
-reduction remains stuck. In contrast, our machine directly
generates a well-formed argument: we represent a
\lambda
-term as a
syntax tree, with de Bruijn indices at the leaves, and applications
and
\lambda
-abstractions at the nodes. We start generating from the
leftmost variable, and we then go left to right, meaning that in an
application, we create the term in function position before the
argument. These choices are completely arbitrary, as doing the
opposite—starting from the rightmost variable and go right to
left—is also possible. To be sure that we produce a valid, closed,

\lambda
-term, we have to check that each de Bruijn index
n
has at
least
n+1

\lambda
-abstractions enclosing it, and that each
application node has two children.

To do so, we consider machine states with four components: the term
t
being
constructed, a counter
\kappa
giving the minimal number of

\lambda
-abstractions required to close the term, a stack
\rho
used to build
applications, whose syntax is

\rho\Coloneqq(t,\kappa)\mathop{::}\rho\;\;\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}\;\;\odot

and which is explained in more detail later, and finally the closure
\eta

for which the argument is being built. This last element is never modified by
the building process, and is just used to restart the machine in evaluation mode
when the argument is finished. We distinguish two kinds of states: the index
state
\langle n,\rho,\eta\rangle_{\mathsf{ind}}
, where only de Bruijn indices can be built, and
the term state
\langle t,\kappa,\rho,\eta\rangle_{\mathsf{tm}}
, where any term can be produced. The
transitions for these states are given in Figure
4
, and we call
the extension of the KAM with these transitions the AB machine.

\displaystyle\langle\lambda.t,e,[]\rangle_{\mathcal{}\mathsf{ev}}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\xmapsto{\makebox[12.51709pt]{\scriptsize$\ocirc$}}}\langle 0,\odot,(t,e)\rangle_{\mathsf{ind}}

(
Arg
)

\displaystyle\langle n,\rho,\eta\rangle_{\mathsf{ind}}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\xmapsto{\makebox[12.51709pt]{\scriptsize$\boxplus$}}}\langle n+1,\rho,\eta\rangle_{\mathsf{ind}}

(
Suc
)

\displaystyle\langle n,\rho,\eta\rangle_{\mathsf{ind}}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\xmapsto{\makebox[12.51709pt]{\scriptsize$\boxdot$}}}\langle n,n+1,\rho,\eta\rangle_{\mathsf{tm}}

(
Var
)

\displaystyle\langle t,\kappa+1,\rho,\eta\rangle_{\mathsf{tm}}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\xmapsto{\makebox[12.51709pt]{\scriptsize$\lambda$}}}\langle\lambda.t,\kappa,\rho,\eta\rangle_{\mathsf{tm}}

(
Lambda
)

\displaystyle\langle t,0,\rho,\eta\rangle_{\mathsf{tm}}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\xmapsto{\makebox[12.51709pt]{\scriptsize$\lambda$}}}\langle\lambda.t,0,\rho,\eta\rangle_{\mathsf{tm}}

(
Lambda0
)

\displaystyle\langle t,\kappa,\rho,\eta\rangle_{\mathsf{tm}}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\xmapsto{\makebox[12.51709pt]{\scriptsize$\leftVdash$}}}\langle 0,(t,\kappa)\mathop{::}\rho,\eta\rangle_{\mathsf{ind}}

(
AppFun
)

\displaystyle\langle s,\kappa_{1},(t,\kappa_{2})\mathop{::}\rho,\eta\rangle_{\mathsf{tm}}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\xmapsto{\makebox[12.51709pt]{\scriptsize$@$}}}\langle t\;s,\mathsf{max}(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}),\rho,\eta\rangle_{\mathsf{tm}}

(
App
)

\displaystyle\langle t,0,\odot,(s,e)\rangle_{\mathsf{tm}}

\displaystyle\mathbin{\xmapsto{\makebox[12.51709pt]{\scriptsize$\medstar$}}}\langle s,(t,\epsilon)\mathop{::}e,[]\rangle_{\mathcal{}\mathsf{ev}}

(
Restart
)

Figure 4.
AB machine: argument generation

The transition
Arg
starts the building process when we reach
a
\lambda
-abstraction in evaluation mode with the empty continuation

[]
. We begin with the index 0 and with the empty stack
\odot
. The
value of the index can then be increased with the transition

Suc
. When we reach the needed value for the index, the
transition
Var
switches to the term mode, indicating that we
need at least
n+1

\lambda
-abstractions to close the term. We use two modes
to prevent a
Suc
transition on a term which is not an index.

In term mode, we can add
\lambda
-abstractions to the term,
decreasing
\kappa
if
\kappa>0
with transition
Lambda
, or
leaving
\kappa
at
0
with transition
Lambda0
; the
abstractions we introduce when
\kappa=0
do not bind any variable. Once
we are done building a term
t
in function position of an
application, we use transition
AppFun
to build the argument

s
. We start again in index mode, but we store on top of
\rho
the
term
t
with its counter
\kappa_{2}
. When we finish
s
with a counter

\kappa_{1}
, we build the application with transition
App
,
which takes the maximum of
\kappa_{1}
and
\kappa_{2}
as the new minimal
number of
\lambda
-abstractions needed above
t\;s
. Note that
the
App
transition is allowed only if
\rho
is not empty,
meaning that at least one
AppFun
has been done
before. Finally, we can conclude the term building process with
transition
Restart
only if
\kappa=0
, meaning that all the
variables of the term are bound, and if
\rho
is empty, meaning that
there is no application waiting to be finished.

{exa}

Figure
5
presents how we generate the term

\lambda.\lambda.(\lambda.\underline{0})\;(1\;\lambda.0)
; we start with
the underlined 0.

\begin{array}[]{cccccccc}\mathbin{\xmapsto{\makebox[12.51709pt]{\scriptsize$\boxdot$}}}\mathbin{\xmapsto{\makebox[12.51709pt]{\scriptsize$\lambda$}}}&\langle\lambda.0,0,(1,2)\mathop{::}(\lambda.0,0)\mathop{::}\odot,\eta\rangle_{\mathsf{tm}}&\mathbin{\xmapsto{\makebox[12.51709pt]{\scriptsize$@$}}}&\langle 1\;{\lambda.0},2,(\lambda.0,0)\mathop{::}\odot,\eta\rangle_{\mathsf{tm}}\\
